# Proposed OEIS comments: A185925 to A197202

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A185925

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A185925>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [8411](../papers/8001-8500/8411-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 6*a(n-1) + 81*a(n-2) + 206*a(n-3) + 144*a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: 64*x*(4 + 29*x + 64*x^2 + 42*x^3) / (1 - 6*x - 81*x^2 - 206*x^3 - 144*x^4). - _Colin Barker_, Apr 17 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 256 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A185966

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A185966>  
Result obtained: **26 August 2026**  
Paper: [9694](../papers/9501-10000/9694-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: 6*n*(n+1)*a(n) -n*(n-14)*a(n-1) +2*n*(14*n-19)*a(n-2) -4*(n-2)*(17*n-48)*a(n-3) +6*(2*n-5)*(n-4)*a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Nov 15 2012

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the recurrence already stated on the entry, which is not labelled a conjecture: in the Ore algebra Q(n)[N], N the shift, the conjectured operator is a left multiple of that one, so it annihilates the sequence as well. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```

```

## A186940

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A186940>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [9354](../papers/9001-9500/9354-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: 3*(n+1)*a(n) +(10-17*n)*a(n-1) -6*(n+1)*a(n-2) +10*(2*n-1)*a(n-3) +9*(1-n)*a(n-4) +(n-2)*a(n-5) =0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Nov 17 2011

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 3, so the recurrence holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A187252

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A187252>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [8997](../papers/8501-9000/8997-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture D-finite with recurrence a(n) +(-2*n-3)*a(n-1) +(n+7)*(n-1)*a(n-2) +4*(-n^2+2*n+1)*a(n-3) +4*(n-3)^2*a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jul 22 2022

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The e.g.f. above is not algebraic, but it lies in a finitely generated module over Q(x) spanned by powers of a logarithm times powers of an exponential, and that module is closed under differentiation, so a(n) is P-recursive. Reducing the conjectured operator inside it leaves a polynomial, and the recurrence holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A188312

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A188312>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [9430](../papers/9001-9500/9430-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: (n+1)*a(n) +(3-4*n)*a(n-1) + (7-6*n)*a(n-2) -a(n-3) +(n-4)*a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Nov 15 2011

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 2, so the recurrence holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A188707

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A188707>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3376](../papers/3001-3500/3376-PROOF.pdf)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 8 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A189145

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A189145>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2324](../papers/2001-2500/2324-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) -3*a(n-2) +6*a(n-3) -6*a(n-5) +3*a(n-6) -3*a(n-7) +a(n-8).
> Empirical: G.f. -x*(1-2*x+4*x^2+x^3+3*x^5+x^7-6*x^4-3*x^6) / ( (x-1)*(1+x)*(x^2-3*x+1)*(x^4+3*x^2+1) ). - _R. J. Mathar_, Oct 15 2011

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The count is a permanent: the cells are permuted and each one moves by one of the offsets the name lists. Number the cells in row-major order and let each choose its image; an offset moves a cell by a bounded number of places in that order, so all that has to be carried is which images inside a window of 11 consecutive positions are already taken. That makes a(n) a walk count on 24 states, hence C-finite of order at most that, and checking the recurrence above is a finite exact computation that holds for n > 7. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The count is a permanent: the cells are permuted and each one moves by one of the offsets the name lists. Number the cells in row-major order and let each choose its image; an offset moves a cell by a bounded number of places in that order, so all that has to be carried is which images inside a window of 15 consecutive positions are already taken. That makes a(n) a walk count on 1 254 states, hence C-finite of order at most that, and checking the recurrence above is a finite exact computation that holds for n > 57. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A189176

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A189176>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [9540](../papers/9501-10000/9540-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: (n+1)*a(n) + 3*(1-3*n)*a(n-1) + 10*(2*n-3)*a(n-2) = 0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Nov 15 2011

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 2, so the recurrence holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A189247

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A189247>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3260](../papers/3001-3500/3260-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 6*a(n-1) + 12*a(n-2) - 16*a(n-3).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(1 + 6*x - 8*x^2) / (1 - 6*x - 12*x^2 + 16*x^3). - _Colin Barker_, Mar 01 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The count is a permanent: the cells are permuted and each one moves by one of the offsets the name lists. Number the cells in row-major order and let each choose its image; an offset moves a cell by a bounded number of places in that order, so all that has to be carried is which images inside a window of 7 consecutive positions are already taken. That makes a(n) a walk count on 10 states, hence C-finite of order at most that, and checking the recurrence above is a finite exact computation that holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A189644

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A189644>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3044](../papers/3001-3500/3044-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 9*a(n-1) - 6*a(n-2) - 8*a(n-3) + 16*a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(3 + 6*x - 16*x^2 + 16*x^3) / (1 - 9*x + 6*x^2 + 8*x^3 - 16*x^4). - _Colin Barker_, May 02 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The count is a permanent: the cells are permuted and each one moves by one of the offsets the name lists. Number the cells in row-major order and let each choose its image; an offset moves a cell by a bounded number of places in that order, so all that has to be carried is which images inside a window of 9 consecutive positions are already taken. That makes a(n) a walk count on 25 states, hence C-finite of order at most that, and checking the recurrence above is a finite exact computation that holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A190166

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: (-n+1)*a(n) +(3*n-4)*a(n-1) +2*(-n+1)*a(n-2) +3*(n-2)*a(n-3) +2*(-n+3)*a(n-4) +(3*n-8)*a(n-5) +(-n+3)*a(n-6)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Apr 09 2019

```

Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 3, so the recurrence holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A194772

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, May 05 2018: (Start)

## A194932

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A194932>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3391](../papers/3001-3500/3391-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 171*n - 127 for n>2.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(64 + 89*x + 16*x^2 + 2*x^3) / (1 - x)^2. - _Colin Barker_, May 05 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true, and it is exact rather than eventual guesswork. The shape is fixed and it is the alphabet 0..n that grows, so this is not a walk count. The condition only ever compares two entries, so adding a constant to every entry keeps an array admissible: the arrays fall into translation classes, and a class whose largest and smallest entries differ by s contributes max(0, n+1-s) arrays with entries in 0..n. There are finitely many classes, because any two cells are joined by a path of at most D edges and each edge moves the value by at most d, so no class has s > d*D. For n at least that bound every term is positive and a(n) is exactly linear, so computing a(n) at two points fixes it for good. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A195956

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A195956>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [7862](../papers/7501-8000/7862-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = a(n-1) +3*a(n-3) -a(n-4) -2*a(n-6).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(1 + 5*x^2 - 4*x^3 - 2*x^4 - 8*x^5) / ((1 - x)*(1 + x + x^2)*(1 - x - 2*x^3)). - _Colin Barker_, May 08 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 625 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 7. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
